the chain-sum channel routes here too, its ends and intermediates ledger-bounded site-term combinations. \emph{Demand:} the match-exactness core (Lemmas~\ref{dich:lem:M}--\ref{dich:lem:S}), the drift closure (Theorem~\ref{dich:thm:D}), the survivor supply bound (Lemma~\ref{dich:lem:INH}), and the atom obligation law (Theorem~\ref{dich:thm:ATOM}) price every path by which a separator of $\mathrm{rev}(\mathcal D(k;3))$ reaches its mirror depth: the exact top-scale window (a scale slot at full consumption, or an exact deficit value -- supply), the mass debt (top-scale, re-paid through Payment), or the survivor supply. \emph{Count:} by the disjunctive counting corollary (Corollary~\ref{dich:cor:counting}), $k \le 4\,\#\mathrm{S}(E) + 2\,\#\mathrm{C}(E) + C' \cdot c\,\#\mathrm{S}(E)$: $E$ fails beyond $k > c_{E}\,\#\mathrm{S}(E) + c'_{E}$, linear in the pass count. The bound is tight up to the polynomial: the fixed-structure engine reverses $k$ separators with $\Theta(k)$ pass depth and $\Theta(k^{2})$ size.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{The descent lemmas.}

\begin{lemma}[sweep normal form]
    \label{dich:lem:sweep}
    Fix a pass $[\,A/B\,]F$ and an input $w$ on which it is defined, with values $A = A(w)$, $B = B(w) \ne \epsilon$, $F = F(w)$. The scan fires $t \ge 0$ times, at the greedy leftmost-disjoint occurrences of $B$ in $F$ in order, and
    \begin{equation*}
        \mathrm{out} = q_0\,A\,q_1\,A\cdots A\,q_t, \qquad
        F = q_0\,B\,q_1\,B\cdots B\,q_t,
    \end{equation*}
    the same $A$ at every firing, the remnants $q_i$ in input order. One pass is a \emph{uniform interleave}: site-specific output can come only from distinct pass nodes or from remnants.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
    The semantics scans $F$ once, left to right, takes the leftmost match first, and never rescans inserted text; disjointness and leftmost-first make the occurrence set the greedy one, and each firing replaces its window by the one value $A$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Final-Pass Lemma; two independent proofs]
    \label{dich:lem:FP}
    Let $w$ have single-$\mathtt b$ separators, positive pairwise distinct gaps and $k \ge 1$, and let $E = [\,A/B\,]F$ satisfy $E(w) = \mathrm{rev}(w)$. Write $p_0, p_1$ for the leading and trailing $\mathtt a$-runs of $B(w)$ (zero if $B(w)$ begins or ends with $\mathtt b$) and $a_1, a_2$ for those of $A(w)$.
    \begin{enumerate}[(i)]
        \item If the pass fires $t \ge 2$ times, $A(w)$ carries at most one $\mathtt b$.
        \item If $t \ge 2$, $A(w) = \mathtt a^{a_1}\mathtt b\,\mathtt a^{a_2}$ and $B(w)$ has a single $\mathtt b$: the output's separator count equals the scrutinee's, and each gap $g$ of $F(w)$ becomes, at the same position,
        \begin{equation*}
            g + \sigma, \qquad
            \sigma \in \{\,0,\; -p_0 + a_1,\; -p_1 + a_2,\; -p_0 - p_1 + a_1 + a_2\,\},
        \end{equation*}
        according to which adjacent separators were windowed; the two end gaps take at most the single adjacent shift ($\sigma \in \{0, -p_0 + a_1\}$ at the left end, $\sigma \in \{0, -p_1 + a_2\}$ at the right); gaps whose image is $0$ vanish, and $0$-gaps may resurface positive.
        \item If $t \ge 2$ and $A(w) = \mathtt a^{m}$ is pure: the output's gaps are \emph{chain sums} -- for each maximal chain of consecutive windowed separators, the sum of the gaps the chain spans, minus the bites taken at its edges and interior flanks, plus $jm$ for the chain's $j$ separators; interior gaps survive exactly when not fully consumed.
        \item If $t \le 1$ the output is $F(w)$ ($t = 0$) or a single splice $q_0\,A\,q_1$.
    \end{enumerate}
    In particular, whenever $E(w) = \mathrm{rev}(w)$, the reversed gap sequence $(i_k, \ldots, i_0)$ is -- up to the pass-fixed shift set of (ii), the chain sums of (iii), or the one splice of (iv) -- an in-order extraction from the gap sequence of $F(w)$: the reversal must already pre-exist in the scrutinee.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
    (i), construction side: suppose $t \ge 2$ and $A(w)$ has at least two $\mathtt b$'s. If some interior gap of $A(w)$ were zero, $A(w)$ would contain $\mathtt{bb}$ and every copy would emit it, while $\mathrm{rev}(w)$ -- like $w$ -- has single separators. So every interior gap is positive, and each of the $t \ge 2$ copies emits each such gap as a complete $\mathtt a$-run of the output, protected by the copy's own separators: the output's gap values would repeat, while $\mathrm{rev}(w)$'s are $w$'s distinct gaps, each once. Counting side (independent): the $t$ disjoint copies sit verbatim in $\mathrm{rev}(w)$; a copy's consecutive separators are consecutive separators of $\mathrm{rev}(w)$, so a copy with two separators and a positive interior forces its interior gap profile onto the same skeleton position of $\mathrm{rev}(w)$ as any other such copy -- gap distinctness permits only one -- contradicting disjointness. (ii)--(iii): window accounting under the sweep normal form. A window whose pattern has a single $\mathtt b$ aligns it with a separator of $F(w)$; its leading $p_0$ letters are the last $p_0$ letters of the gap before that separator and its trailing $p_1$ the first $p_1$ of the gap after (an occurrence needs the room, so no bite can exceed its gap). The replacement's flanks weld onto exactly those bitten gaps: $a_1$ onto the left, $a_2$ onto the right; the string ends have no outer bite. With a single-$\mathtt b$ replacement each windowed separator is replaced by one separator, so the gap positions survive termwise, giving (ii); with a pure replacement the windowed separators vanish and the material on either side of a chain of them fuses with the $jm$ inserted letters, giving (iii). (iv) is immediate from the normal form.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[site distinctness]
    \label{dich:lem:SD}
    If a scrutinee's positive gaps are pairwise distinct and the pattern value has at least two $\mathtt b$'s with at least one positive interior gap, the pass fires at most once. The exception class -- all interior gaps zero, $\mathtt{bb}$-type patterns -- fires multiply.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
    Two windows would put equal interior gap-sequences at two skeleton positions of the scrutinee; positive-gap distinctness forces the same position, contradicting disjointness.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[the flank cap]
    \label{dich:lem:flankcap}
    A flank pattern -- a pattern value with a single $\mathtt b$, say $\mathtt a^{p_0}\mathtt b\,\mathtt a^{p_1}$ -- fully consumes only gaps whose value is $p_0$, $p_1$, or $p_0 + p_1$, whatever its firing count. On $\mathcal D(k;3)$ at most two of these three are powers of $3$: a sum of two powers of $3$ is never a power of $3$ ($3^a + 3^b = 3^a(1 + 3^{b-a})$ with $1 + 3^d$ not divisible by $3$), so \emph{one flank pattern fully consumes at most two deep sizes}.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
    By the window accounting of the Final-Pass Lemma: a gap is bitten only by the windows at its two adjacent separators, in the amounts $p_1$ (the left window's trailing flank) and $p_0$ (the right window's leading flank), each $0$ when that window does not fire there; full consumption requires the total to equal the gap.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[the separation budget]
    \label{dich:lem:SB}
    Call a derivation \emph{merge-free} if every maximal $\mathtt a$-run of every value carries material from at most one input gap, and let $\Phi'(V)$ count the input-adjacent pairs $(t, t+1)$ appearing as \emph{reversed separations} in $V(w)$ -- a separator with gap $t{+}1$'s material immediately left of it, gap $t$'s immediately right. Then $\Phi'(\mathrm{rev}(w)) = k$ for every $w$ with $k$ separators, while merge-free derivations satisfy $\Phi'(E) \le 4\,\#\mathrm{S}(E) + 2\,\#\mathrm{C}(E)$ however often the passes fire. So no merge-free expression computes $\mathrm{rev}$ beyond its size, and any unifier must merge input-gap material into common runs on every input with more separators than that.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
    At a firing, every new reversed separation is pinned by one of the replacement's four boundary-adjacent gap labels, so $t$ firings contribute at most four distinct pairs. The \emph{pollution witness} $[\,(\mathrm{mrg}\,\mathtt b)/\mathtt{aa}\,]\,\mathrm{mrg}$ -- one pass whose every junction separation sits between merged runs carrying all labels, $\Phi' = k$ from a budget of $8$ -- shows that merging is the unbounded flip-creating resource: flip-counting alone can never prove the no-go, and the obstruction must live in run-length exactness.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[the payment theorem]
    \label{dich:lem:PAYMENT}
    For every pass node, $\Phi'(\mathrm{out}) \le \Phi'(F) + \Phi'(R) + 4 + MJ$, where $MJ$ counts the mergey junction events -- separations bearing a reversed pair with a multi-label flanking run -- and the tree total is $\Phi'(E) \le 4\,\#\mathrm{S} + 2\,\#\mathrm{C} + \sum_v MJ(v)$. Moreover every mergey junction flip that \emph{survives} to the final output must be paid: at creation the flanking runs carry foreign site terms (that is what makes them multi-label); the final output's runs are exact singletons; so the foreign terms must be stripped by downstream \emph{exact deep cuts} at inter-term boundaries, each priced by the tuning constraint. The replication escape -- kept material re-supplied by a copy -- defers the payment into the copy, and that deferral is closed by Escape~A below (the copies' picks must be value-stratified, forcing the deep-rich structure into the replacement itself). Consequently, with the demand side below,
    \begin{equation*}
        k \;\le\; 4\,\#\mathrm{S}(E) + 2\,\#\mathrm{C}(E) + C' \cdot \#\{\text{tuned deep cuts}\} \;=\; O(|E|).
    \end{equation*}

\end{lemma}

\begin{lemma}[decomposition]
    \label{dich:lem:DECOMP}
    If $E(w) = A(w) \cdot B(w) = \mathrm{rev}(w)$ then $A(w)$ is the reversal of a suffix and $B(w)$ of a prefix, and inductively the fixed concatenation tree's leaves reverse a partition of $w$ into consecutive intervals in reverse order; by pigeonhole some leaf's sub-tree reverses an interval with unboundedly many separators, and its top must be a pass node -- $X$ only reverses separator-free intervals, where reversal is the identity. This reduces the unification question to pass-topped trees on unbounded-separator intervals, exactly where the Final-Pass Lemma, site distinctness, and the budget apply.
\end{lemma}

\paragraph{The descent.}
Suppose $E$ computes $\mathrm{rev}$ on inputs with unboundedly many separators. By Lemma~\ref{dich:lem:DECOMP} some pass-topped sub-expression reverses such inputs; work at its top pass $[\,A/B\,]F$ on $\mathcal D(k;3)$, $k$ large. The Final-Pass Lemma applies: either the pass is a splice or single firing (a multi-$\mathtt b$ pattern on a distinct-run scrutinee fires at most once, by Lemma~\ref{dich:lem:SD}) and the reversed gap sequence pre-exists in $F$ up to one block edit; or the pass fires multiply with a single-$\mathtt b$ or pure replacement, and the reversed sequence is an in-order extraction from $F$'s gaps up to the shift set or the chain sums. Either way the descent recurses into the scrutinee $F = [\,A'/B'\,]F'$: the extraction's elements come from $F'$'s remnants, in $F'$-order, or from the copies of $A'$, in copy order. Two routes could let the copies carry the reversal; both die, and the lemmas below are what pins each.

\begin{proposition}[Escape A: stratified picks]
    \label{dich:escapeA}
    Copies of a multi-gap $A'$ could donate one element each, values stratified across copies -- copy $c$'s picks all exceed copy $c{+}1$'s, since they sit at strictly decreasing positions of the output. The kill is value-range disjointness: the kept pieces from different copies occupy pairwise disjoint value ranges, so $A'(\mathcal D(k;3))$'s interior must contain that many distinct deep values -- by the truncation ledger (Theorem~\ref{thm:TL}), site terms -- as contiguous stretches; $A'$ is itself reversal-rich, and the descent recurses into it unchanged. This same stratification is what closes the replication deferral in the payment theorem (Lemma~\ref{dich:lem:PAYMENT}). \emph{Status: analysis.} The stratification phenomenon is real: the counterexample class $[\,X/\mathtt b\,]X$ has decreasing-subsequence length growing with $k$ at pass depth $2$, which is why the descent carries value-range disjointness rather than subsequence counts -- the subsequence bound alone was refuted by this very class.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proposition}[Escape B: phase carving]
    \label{dich:escapeB}
    The copies could be of an increasing $A'$, with the final pass's windows carving a different run out of each copy, the carve depth controlled by the greedy phase -- the remnant lengths between firings. The kill is tuning, in the term-unit form. To keep the run $3^j$ of a copy, the windows must consume the adjacent material, whose gap sizes are the dominated powers. By the flank cap (Lemma~\ref{dich:lem:flankcap}) a flank pattern fully consumes at most two deep sizes, whatever its firing count; multi-run patterns fire at most once on distinct-run scrutinees (Lemma~\ref{dich:lem:SD}); and by the truncation ledger (Theorem~\ref{thm:TL}) the padded flanks ($\alpha S + \beta$ plus terms) and product flanks (degree $\ge 2$) are top-scale -- they fully consume any deep run they reach and cannot partially carve it -- so partial deep carving is confined to constant and tweak flanks, whose slivers are off-target (a sum of two powers of $3$ is never a power of $3$). The unit of tuning is the \emph{site term}: each distinct deep term demands its own tuned flank, and a fixed pattern has finitely many runs, each carrying at most $M_V$ site references (the term ledger): still $\Omega(k)$ patterns, i.e.\ $\Omega(k)$ pass nodes. The descent also self-identifies: the phase sequence needed to walk a carve from $3^k$ down to $3^0$ is the power sequence itself, so the deeper level must encode the reversal as remnant lengths, and the recursion terminates at $X$, whose gaps increase. \emph{Status: analysis.} The merge-catalysed deletion -- a pad creating an unbounded run next to a chosen separator, then one pinned flank -- is the one resource that beats the flank-size bound at a chosen site, and it consumes one boundary per pass: the fixed-structure engine's $\Theta(k)$ pass depth is exactly this cost, and the proved supply lemmas plus the demand side are what pin it merge-sensitively.
\end{proposition}

\paragraph{The truncation ledger.}

\begin{theorem}[Truncation Ledger over $\mathcal D(k;3)$]
\label{thm:TL}
Fix a substitution expression $V$ over the two-letter alphabet
$\{a,b\}$, and let $\mathcal D(k;3)=a^{3^0}ba^{3^1}\cdots ba^{3^k}$
and $S=(3^{k+1}-1)/2$. Let $d$ be the $S$-depth of $V$ (the maximum
nesting of substitution nodes), $\#S(V)$ its number of substitution
nodes, and $M_V$ the ledger bound $8\,\#S(V)+O_V(1)$ of clause
{\rm(ii)} below. Collect the pattern and tiling moduli occurring in
$V$ -- the lengths of the pattern values whose segments tile runs, and
the moduli of their window arithmetic -- and set
\begin{align*}
T_V&=\operatorname{lcm}\Bigl(\{2\}\cup
\{\operatorname{ord}_p(3):\ \text{$p$ such a modulus, }\gcd(p,3)=1\}\\
&\quad\cup\{p:\ \text{$p$ such a modulus, }\gcd(p,3)=1\}\Bigr),
\end{align*}
where $\operatorname{ord}_p(3)$ is the multiplicative order of $3$
modulo $p$; a modulus divisible by $3$ imposes no order, its residue
dependence vanishing past a $V$-fixed site index (eventual
dominance). Then every maximal monochromatic run of
$V(\mathcal D(k;3))$ -- of either letter -- has value
\[
v=\sum_{i\le M}q_i\,3^{e_i}+(Ak+B)+\beta,
\]
where:
\begin{enumerate}
\item[\rm(i)] \emph{strata.} The slopes $q_i\in\mathbb Q$ and the
affine data $A,B$ are $V$-fixed on each residue class of $k$ modulo
$T_V$; each exponent $e_i$ is \emph{anchored}: a sum of at most
three terms of the forms $k-c$, $\iota-c'$, $c''$ with $V$-fixed
offsets, where the indices $\iota$ are the ambient family indices
of the run's emission path -- at most $d$ of them, each an input
site or affine in the input sites; for ambient arity one, the input
sites $0,\dots,k$.
\item[\rm(ii)] \emph{term ledger.} The site-reference count obeys
$M\le M_V$; the first and last runs satisfy $M\le 2$, with only
top-anchored (and products of two top-anchored) exponents.
\item[\rm(iii)] \emph{junk.} $|\beta|=O(k)$, and $\beta$ collects
to a $k$-affine part plus a $T_V$-periodic part.
\end{enumerate}
Moreover the run \emph{profile} is pinned in the same sense, as a
\emph{two-color} object. The output text decomposes uniquely as an
alternating sequence of maximal runs
$a_0\,b_0\,a_1\,b_1\cdots b_{m-1}\,a_m$ with $b_i\ge1$, interior
$a_i\ge1$, $a_0,a_m\ge0$; run-length decoding is a bijection on
texts, while the $a$-lengths alone are not (the remark below).
There is a \emph{profile grammar}: on each residue class of $k$
modulo $T_V$ -- at most $T_V$ of them -- a $V$-fixed directive tree
of $N_V$ nodes ($N_V\le 3^{d}\,(2|V|+1)$ whenever every fired set
is one-dimensionally indexed, and $N_V\le 2^{|V|^{2}}$ in general),
whose directives are single values, masked index families -- $j\in[\ell(k)..h(k)]$
with $\ell,h$ affine in $k$, refined by a $V$-fixed Boolean
combination of residue tests $j\equiv r\pmod T$ -- and outer
families instantiating sub-lists. Its expansion, over forms as in
(i)--(iii), is exactly the two-color run sequence. Writing
$\Delta_V\le|V|$ for the tree's nesting depth -- additive under
replication, $\Delta(S(R,P,F))\le\Delta(F)+\Delta(R)$ -- the
expansion has at most $2\,N_V\,(k+2)^{\Delta_V}$ entries up to
$V$-fixed constants (the leading $2$ is $b$-run bookkeeping,
absorbable if runs are counted per color), and may have length
$\Theta(k^{\Delta_V})$: $V=[u/b]\,u$ with $u=[x/b]\,x$ ($x$ the
input) has $\Delta_V=4$ and $k^{4}+1$ runs. Each emitted entry is a
merge of at most $d+2$ form pieces (the Merge Lemma, uniformly over
both colors), so the expansion carries at most
$2\,N_V\,(k+2)^{\Delta_V}\,(d+2)\,M_V$ form terms in all. Every
tree is $k$-independent.
\end{theorem}

\begin{remark}
\label{rem:TLprof}
\textnormal{(1)~The two-color refinement is necessary. Passes may
output consecutive $b$'s, and then the $a$-lengths alone both fail
to determine the text ($ab$ and $abb$ have the same collapsed
$a$-runs) and can be exponentially long in $k$: $[b/a^4]\mathcal
D(k;3)$ has $\Theta(3^{k})$ $a$-runs, all but $k+1$ of them empty,
so no $k$-independent grammar can pin them. Its two-color profile
has $2k+1$ entries, and its $b$-entries are anchored exponentials
with periodic corrections -- forms of class (i)--(iii).
(2)~The periodic masks genuinely occur, with period an order
$\operatorname{ord}_p(3)$ of the inner tiling modulus $p$. For
$V=[b/\mathit{bab}]\,[b/a^4]\,\mathcal D(k;3)$ the pattern
$\mathit{bab}$ fires exactly at the sites $j$ with $3^j\equiv
1\pmod 4$, i.e.\ at the residue class $\{j\ \mathrm{even}:\
2\le j\le k-1\}$ of period $\operatorname{ord}_4(3)=2$ -- a residue
class, not an interval. The surviving family is the complementary
class; each firing deletes its interior run and merges the two
flanking $b$-runs with the inserted letter into a single $b$-entry
(three Merge pieces, within the $d+2$ budget); and the two cells
$k\bmod 2$ carry structurally different tails. The modulus $7$
behaves identically with period $\operatorname{ord}_7(3)=6$: the
fired set of $[b/\mathit{bab}]\,[b/a^{7}]\,\mathcal D(k;3)$ is
$\{j:\ 6\mid j,\ 1\le j\le k-1\}$, its surviving family the
complement class $j\not\equiv 0\pmod 6$.}
\end{remark}

\noindent On $\mathcal D(k;3)$ the site terms are the powers $3^t$, and the reading used below is: every term carrying $S$ to degree $\ge 1$ is a fixed rational multiple of $S$, hence top-scale ($S > 3^k$), and the products of site terms with such factors likewise -- so a \emph{deep} value is a constant-coefficient combination of at most $M_V$ site terms, each a ledger entry. Two fixed expressions refute every narrower form. The padded extraction (the \emph{leak}) $E_{\mathrm{leak}} = [\,(\mathrm{mrg}\,\mathtt b)/\mathtt b\,]X$ has runs exactly $(S + 3^0, \ldots, S + 3^{k-1}, 3^k)$ -- site-plus-global sums, neither a bounded tweak of a single gap nor a pinned affine function of $S$. The pollution (the \emph{product}) $E_{\mathrm{prod}} = [\,\mathrm{mrg}/\mathtt{aa}\,]X$ has interior runs exactly $S\,(3^j - 1)/2 + 1$ and last run $1 + \lfloor 3^k/2 \rfloor \cdot S$ -- count-times-global products, a class beyond the ledger's original three. The $k$-affine stratum is irreducible: the halver $[\,\epsilon/\mathtt b\,][\,\mathtt a/\mathtt{aa}\,]X$ equals $\mathtt a^{(S+k+1)/2}$ on $\mathcal D(k;3)$, the $+k/2$ not expressible as any $Q(S)$. And the slopes are rational with $V$-fixed denominators -- tiling corrections $|R|_{\mathtt a}/p$, witnessed at base 2 by the tripler $[\,\mathtt a/\mathtt{aaa}\,]X$ whose runs $(2^j + 2)/3$ (even $j$) and $(2^j + 4)/3$ (odd $j$) are non-dyadic -- which is itself load-bearing: non-dyadic supply never equals dyadic demand (cross-multiplication: $s \nmid 3^{\Delta} 2^{d}$). The constants: the run-level ledger is $M_V \le 8\,\#\mathrm{S}(V) + O_V(1)$ with the first and last runs at $M \le 2$, and the per-form piece count is $(d+2)\,M_V$.

\paragraph{The supply side.}

\begin{lemma}[slope-pinning]
    \label{dich:lem:L2.1}
    An exact re-separation at $\mathcal D(k;3)$ -- a separator whose flanking runs carry, in reversed order, the material of input gaps $3^u, \ldots, 3^v$ -- requires the interval sum $\sum_{i \in [u..v]} 3^i = \alpha S + \mathrm{err}$ with $\alpha = 3^{v-k}$ and $|\mathrm{err}| \le (3^u-1)/2$; an exact cut at top-offset $d$ requires dyadic slope $3^{-d}$; the tweak channel (a flank within $O(1)$ of a single gap) covers only $O(1)$ offsets from an end; and the powers of $3$ are pairwise distinct and power-separated.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
    The interval identity is the geometric sum: $I(u,v) = 3^{v-k} S - (3^{u} - 3^{v-k})/2$, the deficit bounded by the bottom scale; single interval sums are pairwise distinct and never within $O(1)$ of a power except when $u = v$ (the run itself: the tweak channel); so an exactness cut at interface $(u,v)$ is realized only by a computed value of dyadic slope $3^{v-k}$, the bounded error absorbable by tweaks only when $u$, or $k - v$, is $O(1)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[per-node supply]
    \label{dich:lem:L2.2}
    Each firing cuts only at its window's two ends, with the same amounts at every firing; interior pattern runs are demands (by match anchoring). So one pass supplies at most four cut values, unconditionally (no family hypothesis).
\end{lemma}

\paragraph{The demand side.}
The supply lemmas cap what a fixed expression can cut exactly; the demand side prices what reversal needs. Fix a pass $[\,R/P\,]$ on $\mathcal D(k;3)$ whose pattern value carries a single $\mathtt b$: $P = \mathtt a^{p_0}\mathtt b\,\mathtt a^{p_1}$ (multi-$\mathtt b$ patterns fire at most once on distinct-run scrutinees, Lemma~\ref{dich:lem:SD}). Let $R$'s value have mass $M_R$ and internal separator-prefixes $\rho_j$ (the $\mathtt a$-mass before its $j$-th internal separator); the net per-firing mass change is $\Delta = M_R - p_0 - p_1$. The \emph{channel} of an $R$-internal separator $j$ is its plant-depth sequence across firings -- an arithmetic sequence with constant $c = \rho_j - p_0$ and drift $\Delta$. A plant is \emph{anchored} when its depth is a non-inherited interval sum; the mirror targets are $I(t{+}1,k)$, all at least $3^k$.

\begin{lemma}[the two-sided match]
    \label{dich:lem:M}
    On $\mathcal D(k;3)$: (i) separator $\sigma$ (between runs $\sigma$, $\sigma{+}1$) fires iff $3^{\sigma} - \beta_{\sigma} p_1 \ge p_0$ and $3^{\sigma+1} \ge p_1$, where $\beta_{\sigma} = 1$ if $\sigma \ge 1$ and $\sigma - 1$ fired (the double-bite bookkeeping: a fired window's trailing flank eats into run $\sigma{+}1$'s head, so the next window's leading flank sees a shortened run). (ii) Surviving run $j$ has length $3^{j} - [j{-}1\ \mathrm{fired}]\,p_1 - [j\ \mathrm{fired}]\,p_0$; at each fired $\sigma$ the separator is replaced by a copy of $R$. (iii) The $j$-th planted separator of the copy inserted at firing $\sigma$ sits at depth $\mathrm{BotSum}(\sigma) + t_{\sigma}\Delta - p_0 + \rho_j$, $t_{\sigma}$ the number of earlier firings; a surviving (inherited) separator $j$ sits at $\mathrm{BotSum}(j) + t_j \Delta$. (iv) All plants of firing $\sigma$ lie in $[\mathrm{BotSum}(\sigma{-}1) + t_{\sigma}\Delta,\ \mathrm{BotSum}(\sigma) + t_{\sigma}\Delta + M_R]$: each firing plants only within one prefix-ladder step (plus $R$'s mass) of its drift-shifted separator.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
    (i)--(ii): induction along the greedy leftmost-first scan; the only subtlety is the double bite on a run between two fired separators. (iii): the $\mathtt a$-material before the plant is (runs $0..\sigma{-}1$'s remnants) $+$ (earlier copies) $-$ (this window's leading bite) $+$ ($R$'s prefix) -- each earlier firing contributes $-(p_0 + p_1)$ of remnant and $+M_R$ of copy, i.e.\ $+\Delta$. (iv): $\mathrm{BotSum}(\sigma) - \mathrm{BotSum}(\sigma{-}1) = 3^{\sigma}$ and $\rho_j \in [0, M_R]$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[realizability]
    \label{dich:lem:FIT}
    For every channel: $-p_0 \le c = \rho_j - p_0 \le \Delta + p_1$, and at every firing $p_0 \le 3^{\sigma}$ and $p_1 \le 3^{\sigma+1}$. Consequently, for a channel whose first anchored hit is at firing $s_0$ (a fortiori whose first firing is $\le s_0$): $c \le \Delta + 3^{s_0+1}$ and $-c \le 3^{s_0}$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
    $\rho_j \in [0, M_R]$ and $M_R = \Delta + p_0 + p_1$; the flank bounds are Lemma~\ref{dich:lem:M}(i) at each firing -- the leading flank must fit run $\sigma$, the trailing flank run $\sigma{+}1$. These are necessary conditions, and the rigidity below is proved under them alone: a channel violating them is an arithmetic ghost, realizable by no pass.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[channel rigidity]
    \label{dich:lem:K2}
    Every realizable channel with $\Delta = 0$ and at least two anchored hits is exactly the bottom-fixed ladder: $c = -\mathrm{BotSum}(u{-}1)$, hits at every $\sigma \in [u, k)$, targets $I(u, \sigma)$ -- magnitude-capped below $3^k$, hence never a mirror target. Every other realizable channel with $\Delta = 0$ has at most one anchored hit.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
    The ladder is consistent: $\mathrm{BotSum}(\sigma) - \mathrm{BotSum}(u{-}1) = I(u, \sigma)$, an identity (the $3$-letter pass $[\,\mathtt{baa}/\mathtt{aba}\,]X$ realizes $u = 1$). Conversely, two real anchored hits at firings $\sigma_1 < \sigma_2$ with targets $I(u_1, v_1)$, $I(u_2, v_2)$ give, by Lemma~\ref{dich:lem:M}(iii), the pair equation $I(u_2, v_2) - I(u_1, v_1) = I(\sigma_1{+}1, \sigma_2)$. The interval kernel classifies its solutions: $I(u,v)$ has ternary digits $1$ exactly at scales $u..v$ (a repunit block), so repunit blocks add digitwise with digits $\le 2$ and no carries -- a gap between two blocks leaves a $0$-digit inside the union, an overlap a digit $2$ -- and the solutions are exactly the ladder rungs ($u_1 = u_2$, $v_i = \sigma_i$) and the cross-pairs ($u_1 = \sigma_2{+}1$, $u_2 = \sigma_1{+}1$, common top $v$). Every cross-pair is a ghost at the real firing $\sigma_1$: its constant $c \ge (5 \cdot 3^{\sigma_2} + 1)/2 > 3^{\sigma_2} \ge 3^{\sigma_1+1}$, so the trailing flank there would need $p_1 \ge c > 3^{\sigma_1+1}$, violating the fit (Lemma~\ref{dich:lem:FIT}). A third hit over-determines the cross-pair system; the ladder's rungs are pairwise consistent by the identity.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[the mirror forcing]
    \label{dich:lem:S}
    A realizable channel planting at a mirror target $I(u,k)$ from firing $\sigma$ (with $\Delta = 0$) has $\sigma = k{-}1$, and the trailing flank lies in the exact window
    \begin{equation*}
        p_1 \in [\,3^{k} - \mathrm{BotSum}(u{-}1),\ 3^{k}\,],
    \end{equation*}
    with $3^k - p_1 \in [0, \mathrm{BotSum}(u{-}1)]$: top-scale material whose deficit from $3^k$ is bounded by the target's own bottom scale ($\mathrm{BotSum}(u{-}1) < 3^{u}/2$), the leftover of run $k$ after the bite exactly that deficit. The forcing is exclusive: no realizable channel serves a mirror target from $\sigma \le k{-}2$. With drift: either the window persists ($|(t{+}1)\Delta| \le \mathrm{BotSum}(u{-}1)$, the deficit still exact and sub-bottom-scale) or the pass mis-books more than the target's bottom scale of mass.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
    $c = I(u,k) - \mathrm{BotSum}(\sigma) = (3^{k+1} - 3^{u} - 3^{\sigma+1} + 1)/2$; if $\sigma \le k{-}2$ then $c > 3^{\sigma+1}$ -- a ghost by Lemma~\ref{dich:lem:FIT}. At $\sigma = k{-}1$: $c = 3^{k} - \mathrm{BotSum}(u{-}1)$ and the fit gives $c \le p_1 \le 3^{k}$, i.e.\ the window; the identity $3^{k} - c = \mathrm{BotSum}(u{-}1)$ is the leftover bound.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[the drift closure]
    \label{dich:thm:D}
    On $\mathcal D(k;3)$, every mirror-target service by any channel, drifting or not, pays one of: the exact top-scale window (Lemma~\ref{dich:lem:S}'s, drift-robust: $p_1 \in [3^{k} - \mathrm{BotSum}(t) - (t{+}1)\Delta,\ 3^{k}]$, deficit $< 3^{t+1}$ -- an exact top-scale pattern value: a scale slot at full consumption, or an exact deficit value; both supply) or the mass debt. Specifically: (D1) a channel hit at a mirror target at firing $\sigma$ satisfies $\sigma = k{-}1$, or $\Delta > 0$ with $(t{+}1)\Delta \ge (3^{k}+1)/2$ -- the drift must have net-created at least half the top scale before the firing; negative drift never serves a mirror target from $\sigma \le k{-}2$. (D2) at $\sigma = k{-}1$: the window persists, or the total drift exceeds half the target's bottom scale -- mass mis-booking. (D3) a channel serving two or more mirror targets pays the D1 debt. (MR) mass routing: a pass with drift $\Delta \ne 0$ and $f$ firings changes the total $\mathtt a$-mass by exactly $f\Delta$; the final output has mass $S$, so $\sum_i f_i \Delta_i = 0$ -- every created unit is destroyed, and the debts must be re-paid by mass-destroying passes whose final exactness is priced by the payment theorem (Lemma~\ref{dich:lem:PAYMENT}) and the per-pattern scale caps.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
    D1: the fit $c \le \Delta + 3^{\sigma+1}$ gives $(t{+}1)\Delta \ge W - 3^{\sigma+1}$ with $W = (3^{k+1} - 3^{u} - 3^{\sigma+1} + 1)/2$; for $\sigma \le k{-}2$, $W \ge (5 \cdot 3^{k-1} + 1)/2$, whence $(t{+}1)\Delta \ge (3^{k}+1)/2$ and $\Delta > 0$. D2 is the fit algebra at $\sigma = k{-}1$ ($3^{k} - p_1 \le 2\,\mathrm{BotSum}(u{-}1) = 3^{u} - 1$ in the window case). D3: one plant per firing per channel, so two services sit at distinct firings, the earlier at $\sigma \le k{-}2$. MR is Lemma~\ref{dich:lem:M}(ii) summed over the tree.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[the survivor supply bound]
    \label{dich:lem:INH}
    In the nested-sweep tree, every separator of the final output is a plant of the outermost pass or a surviving separator of its scrutinee, and recursively: every separator of any intermediate value is a plant of that level's pass or a survivor of its scrutinee; the recursion terminates at the original input separators (the \emph{origin trace}). A final separator at a mirror depth $I(t{+}1,k)$ that is an all-the-way survivor of the original separator $j$ has at most $\mathrm{BotSum}(j) \le (3^{k}-1)/2$ of never-inserted mass before it -- the never-inserted atoms descend injectively into the input prefix, and bites only remove -- hence at least $(3^{k}+1)/2$ of copy-inserted mass before it; and that prefix \emph{is} the exact mirror prefix, so the inserted chunks' sizes and boundaries are exact deep values. At $\Delta = 0$ no survivor sits at any mirror depth at all: $\mathrm{BotSum}(j) \equiv 1 \pmod 3$ while every mirror target is $\equiv 0$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
    Along the survivor's chain, the material before it at each level is that level's copy-mass inserted before it plus the next level's prefix before the corresponding separator, minus bites; the never-inserted atoms descend through the chain prefix at every level without ever lying inside an inserted copy: the level-to-level map on such atoms is injective (each level's prefix is a concatenation of disjoint next-level chunks and copies; a never-inserted atom comes from a unique next-level atom), and bites only remove. So never-inserted mass $\le$ the input prefix mass $\mathrm{BotSum}(j)$ before separator $j$.
\end{proof}

\noindent The supply of exact deep values is governed by the fired-set structure of a single-$\mathtt b$ pass on increasing-run texts.

\begin{lemma}[the fired-set structure]
    \label{dich:lem:FSL}
    On any text with runs $R_0..R_k$ and pattern $\mathtt a^{i}\mathtt b\,\mathtt a^{j}$: the greedy fired set is exactly $c_0 = R_0$; $\mathrm{fire}_{\sigma} \iff c_{\sigma} \ge i$ and $R_{\sigma+1} \ge j$; $c_{\sigma+1} = R_{\sigma+1} - j \cdot [\mathrm{fire}_{\sigma}]$. One-flank patterns ($i = 0$ or $j = 0$) fire at suffixes of the junction order. Two-flank patterns fire at the fitting region -- itself a suffix on strictly increasing runs -- minus isolated holes, each hole immediately after a firing (SKIP-AFTER-BITE), with resumption: after a skip the next junction sees a full run. Consequently, on texts whose runs are interval sums of $\mathcal D(k;3)$: no single-$\mathtt b$ pass fires exactly a proper prefix $\{b_0..b_{m-1}\}$ with $1 \le m \le k{-}1$ (the $k = 2$ edge exception is where the relevant bound is $O(1)$ anyway); none fires exactly an interior block $\{b_u..b_v\}$ with $1 \le u < v \le k{-}2$; the size-one interior exception fires only at $u = k{-}2$, eating more than two-thirds of run $k{-}1$. The class escape: prepending a pad $\mathtt a^{S+1}$ -- making run $0$ the maximum -- is the controlled break of the increasing-run class, deleting exactly one head junction per escape at top-scale cost, the increase restored after each; the class can only be escaped with material exceeding the text's total mass.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
    The closed form is the scan mechanics -- the window at $b_{\sigma}$ consumes the last $i$ atoms of run $\sigma$'s remnant, $b_{\sigma}$, and the first $j$ atoms of run $\sigma{+}1$; the leftmost occurrence is at the first fitting junction. The exclusions are the cascade: a skipped junction after a firing has a bitten run, the resumption fires whenever the fit holds, and the endgame cases contradict super-increase ($R_{k-1} < i + j \le R_u + R_{u+1} < 3^{k} \le R_{k-1}$, and its coarsening version). The class-escape accounting is the engine's own mechanism, made precise in its autopsy below.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[order preservation and the plant trace]
    \label{dich:lem:trace}
    (D1) The remnant-channel atoms of a pass's value -- those descending from the scrutinee without passing through a copy of the replacement -- keep their input order, left to right. (D1\textup{''}-weak) The output runs that are pass-pure and single-label have non-decreasing labels, each label $\lambda$ satisfying $3^{\lambda} \ge$ the run's value, with per-label exhaustion (total length at one label $\le 3^{\lambda}$); the strong one-shared-label form is open and is not used. (The mod-$3$ plant trace) The $t$-th output plant sits at $\mathtt a$-mass $\mathrm{TopSum}(t) \equiv 0 \pmod 3$, while every input $\mathtt b$ sits at $\mathrm{BotSum}(\sigma) \equiv 1 \pmod 3$: no input $\mathtt b$ survives at a correct plant position undisplaced -- every output $\mathtt b$ is routed (a plant event) or displaced by nonzero mass change, independently of the label structure.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
    D1 is the sweep normal form's position flow -- the output is $q_0 R q_1 R \cdots R q_t$ with remnants $q_i$ in $F$-order, concatenation concatenates, $X$ is the identity, constants are fresh -- by induction over the expression tree. D1\textup{''}-weak follows with the slice bound and per-label exhaustion. The mod-$3$ step is the residue computation: $\mathrm{TopSum}(t)$ sums scales $\ge 1$; $\mathrm{BotSum}(\sigma)$ contains the scale-$0$ unit. (An earlier corollary of this lemma -- every output run whose value exceeds $3^{\lambda}$ is copy-routed or merge-assembled, the T2/T3 channels -- was withdrawn: it needed the strong form; nothing else in the composition did.)
\end{proof}

\noindent One autopsy grounds the supply side's constants. On the fixed-structure engine of Theorem~\ref{thm:separators}, the reversal is not a permutation of runs -- output run $0$ (length $3^{k}$) is a merged blob of all the input runs' material, its exact length arithmetic ($S$ minus the preceding prefix-sum), the remaining $k$ output runs are slices of the top input run, all $k$ output $\mathtt b$'s are routed plants at the exact top-anchored sums (at $k = 4$: $81, 108, 117, 120$ with $S = 121$), the $k$ junction differentiations are paid by $k$ box patterns each carrying exactly two anchored-offset values (part-B-tight), and the interior anchored sums are reached only by end-walks -- $\Theta(k)$ deletion nodes per junction, $\Theta(k^{2})$ in all. The end-walk cost is not an artifact of the engine; it is the supply side's floor:

\begin{theorem}[the atom obligation law]
    \label{dich:thm:ATOM}
    Let $T$ be a derivation tree on $\mathcal D(k;3)$ (all pass children evaluated at the original input). Any standalone $\mathtt a$-block of value $I(u,v)$ ($u \ge 1$, or $u = 0$ with $v < k$) demanded in a replacement or pattern position of $T$ sits at depth
    \begin{equation*}
        \ge\ \frac{\min(v, k-u) - 1 - \lfloor \log_3 M \rfloor}{2}
    \end{equation*}
    from the demand point, where $M$ is the raw digit mass of the correction equation at the producing node, $M \le Q\,(8\,\#\mathrm{S} + O_V(1)) + (v - u + 1) + O(\log k)$. For trees with $\#\mathrm{S} = O(k)$: depth $\ge \min(v, k-u)/2 - O(\log k)$ -- $\Omega(\min(v, k{-}u))$ survives.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
    The collected form: the correction equation at the producing node, $\sum_p s_p v_p + t = I(u,v)$ with the pieces the ledger's strata values (Theorem~\ref{thm:TL}), is collected scale by scale into $\sum_d \mu_d 3^{d} = 0$, where $\mu_d$ collects the strata coefficients, the target's repunit digits, and the raw ternary digits of the collected low-scale material $\gamma = \sum_p s_p(A_p k + B_p + \beta_p) + t$ ($\gamma$ names the value of the material inside $\mu$; $|\gamma| = O(k)$ enters only the gap bound $3^{g-1} \le D M$ above the boundary $\log_3(2 D |\gamma|)$). The digit-defect lemma: if $d^{*}$ is the top defect scale and no stratum sits within $g$ scales below it, then $(1/D)\,3^{d^{*}} \le M\,3^{d^{*}-g} + |\gamma|$, so $3^{g-1} \le D M$ above the boundary -- the top of the defect tower is matched by an anchored exponent at that scale, within the log-of-mass gap, and the induction continues downward. The three cases at the worst scale (spanning; carry, a chain of length $g$ requiring $\ge 3^{g}$ raw units concentrated at the origin; count, the multiplicities classified by the shift and length-divisibility laws) each give an anchored exponent with supply obligation $\ge m^{*} - 1 - \lfloor \log_3 (D M) \rfloor$. The mass form: the deficit is the carry-chain length into the worst scale, and $3^{g} \le M$; the smallest violation of the unit bound is at seven pieces ($7\,I(0,1) + 8 = I(2,3)$, the double two-scale carry, $M = 20$; at six or fewer the concentration $8 < 9 = 3^{2}$ is impossible). The structural induction closes exactly at $C = 2$: the demand point pays $1 + (m^{*} - 1)/2 \ge m^{*}/2$, with the base cases the powers (one scale per node) and the $V$-fixed constants.
\end{proof}

\noindent The supply of exact deep values is therefore priced by a trichotomy: (a) \emph{per-pattern scale slots} -- full consumption of deep runs, at most two deep sizes per flank pattern (Lemma~\ref{dich:lem:flankcap}); (b) \emph{deep tuned intermediates} -- the exact deficit and window values and the replacement-internal differences are exact deep values that must be supplied by earlier passes, priced by the atom obligation law (Theorem~\ref{dich:thm:ATOM}); (c) \emph{mergey pollution} -- mass mis-booked beyond the bottom-scale allowance, stripped downstream by exact deep cuts (the payment theorem, Lemma~\ref{dich:lem:PAYMENT}). The counting assembles:

\begin{corollary}[the counting, disjunctive]
    \label{dich:cor:counting}
    Any $E$ computing $\mathrm{rev}(\mathcal D(k;3))$ must place $k$ separators at the mirror depths $I(t{+}1,k)$. Tracing each to its origin: if any target is survivor-served, Lemma~\ref{dich:lem:INH} gives $(3^{k}+1)/2$ of exact top-scale supply, priced $\Omega(k)$ by the atom obligation law and the payment theorem. Otherwise all $k$ are plant-served: by Theorem~\ref{dich:thm:D}(D3) a channel serving two targets pays the $(3^{k}+1)/2$ mass debt (again $\Omega(k)$), so single-service channels dominate -- $k$ distinct channels, each paying the exact top-scale window (a scale slot at full consumption, at most two per pattern, or an exact deficit value: supply) or the mass debt; and a multi-channel pass serving several targets through distinct replacement-internal separators needs replacement-internal run sizes equal to exact mirror differences $(3^{t} - 3^{t'})/2$ -- supply again. Both branches are linear: $k \le f(|E|)$ with $f$ linear. (The origin trace applies at every level; the perturbed-level interface -- plants at scrutinees that are intermediate values -- is stated, not proved here: it is carried by the supply branch, a perturbed-level channel serving two mirror-relevant targets needing exact replacement-internal differences, the single-service perturbed case recursing by the trace to the input level or to survivors.)
\end{corollary}

\noindent The map is complete. An obstruction can hide nowhere at any fixed structure, and at varying structure every path by which a separator could reach its mirror depth is priced: the plant window, the mass debt, or the survivor supply. Reversal is therefore not $L$-reachable -- over any finite alphabet of at least two letters, and by the coding invariance (Theorem~\ref{thm:rev-uniform}) for all of them at once. The base-$2$ boundary records where the price moves rather than whether it is paid: values free, order priced, the same $\Omega(k)$ shape for a different reason; uniform reversal on the telescoping family itself remains open there. What the full question of Section~\ref{sec:variants} inherits is the answer; what remains open there is the once-primitive and the exact criterion $\mathrm{r} \in L$ of the union.

\subsection{The Unary Edge}

Section~\ref{sec:variants} tracks the unary case wherever it differs; the transfer theorem now locates its boundary exactly.

\begin{proposition}
    (\emph{The Boundary $|\Sigma| = 1$})
    \label{prop:unary-edge}
    \begin{enumerate}[(i)]
        \item $c(\mathtt{a}) = \mathtt{ab}$ is good ($\{w\}$ is comma-free iff $w$ is primitive), and every unary-reachable function is the restriction of a binary-reachable one.
        \item There is no injective monoid homomorphism $\{\mathtt{a}, \mathtt{b}\}^* \rightarrow \{\mathtt{a}\}^*$: any $h$ has $h(\mathtt{ab}) = h(\mathtt{a})h(\mathtt{b}) = h(\mathtt{b})h(\mathtt{a}) = h(\mathtt{ba})$ while $\mathtt{ab} \ne \mathtt{ba}$. So no coding-conjugation crosses the $|\Sigma| = 1$ boundary from above, and the hypothesis $|\Sigma|, |\Gamma| \geq 2$ of Theorem~\ref{thm:transfer} cannot be dropped on that side.
        \item Every unary $L$-expression computes a length map in the closure of $\{n \mapsto n,\ n \mapsto k\}$ under pointwise sum (concatenation) and $(g,r,p) \mapsto \big(n \mapsto r(n) \lfloor g(n)/p(n) \rfloor + g(n) \bmod p(n)\big)$ (the pass; the constant case is $n \mapsto i\lfloor n/j \rfloor + n \bmod j$, and $[\,X_1/a\,]X_1$ squares). Every such map is eventually polynomially bounded, so $n \mapsto 2^n$ is unreachable at any size -- the unary face of Theorem~\ref{thm:fp}'s growth bound. The floor-halving $n \mapsto \lfloor n/2 \rfloor$ -- the needle $H$ of Proposition~\ref{prop:unary-once} -- is, we conjecture, absent outright: every single unary pass leaks its residue $n \bmod j$, and subtraction is not among the closures.
    \end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
    (i) is Direction 1 for the coding $c$. (ii) is the displayed computation. (iii): the pass law is the greedy scan of a unary string; the growth bound is an induction -- a pass outputs at most $|R| \cdot \lfloor |E|/|P| \rfloor \cdot |P| + |E| \le |R||E| + |E|$ characters; and the conjecture's mechanism is the pass law itself: each pass leaves its residue $g \bmod p$ in the output, and the closure operations -- pointwise sum and the pass form -- cannot cancel one.
\end{proof}

\section{Recursive Definitions}
\label{sec:recursion}

The \texttt{meow} language recurses: a macro body may call macros, itself included, and the calls may be mutual. This section adds exactly that to the calculus and asks what it buys. The primitive stays raw -- the pass of Definition~\ref{def:subst} over the toolkit of Section~\ref{sec:toolkit}, itself substitution-expressible, with no new constructs -- and everything that varies is the \emph{runtime}: when a sub-expression's value is demanded. Two runtimes span the space between \emph{nothing new} and \emph{everything}, and the pivot turns out to be a clause of Definition~\ref{def:subst} that has been there all along.

\begin{definition}
    (\emph{Recursive programs})
    \label{def:recprog}
    A \emph{program} is a finite list of distinct names $f_1, \dots, f_k$, the $j$-th with arity $m_j$ and body $B_j$ built as in Definition~\ref{def:exp} from $f_j$'s parameters and the constants, extended by one node: the call $f_{j'}(E_1, \dots, E_{m_{j'}})$. Its \emph{call graph} has the edge $j \to j'$ when $B_j$ mentions $f_{j'}$; the program is \emph{recursive} when the graph has a directed cycle, and $C$ is the set of indices that can reach one.
\end{definition}

Two runtimes, in order of increasing laziness:
\begin{enumerate}
    \item \emph{Eager.} Call by value: the denotation is the least fixpoint of $\Phi(\rho)_j \triangleq \llbracket B_j \rrbracket_\rho$ (call nodes read through $\rho$), over tuples of partial functions ordered by graph inclusion.
    \item \emph{Lazy passes.} The one non-strictness the operator itself already possesses, made official: in $[\,R/P\,]\,E$ the pattern and the scrutinee are forced, but $R$ only if $P$ occurs in the value of $E$ -- justified by the operator equation $[\,A/B\,]S = S$ when $B \not\subset S$, \emph{regardless of $A$} (Definition~\ref{def:subst}).
\end{enumerate}

\begin{definition}
    (\emph{The runtimes as dynamics})
    \label{def:dynamics}
    The two runtimes are structural dynamics: rewrite relations on
    closed expressions, the strings being the values.  Write $B_j[X_i := E_i]$
    for the simultaneous substitution of Lemma~\ref{lem:beta}.  The axiom
    schemes, all closed under the runtime's evaluation contexts:
    \begin{mathpar}
        (\mathrm{cat})\;\; \frac{}{W_1\,W_2 \;\to\; W_1W_2}
        \and
        (\mathrm{fire})\;\; \frac{W_P \neq \epsilon \quad W_P \subseteq W_S}
        {[\,W_A/W_P\,]\,W_S \;\to\; [\,W_A/W_P\,](W_S)}
        \and
        (\mathrm{inert}_{=})\;\; \frac{W_P \neq \epsilon \quad W_P \not\subset W_S}
        {[\,W_A/W_P\,]\,W_S \;\to\; W_S}
        \and
        (\mathrm{inert}_{\epsilon})\;\; \frac{W_P \neq \epsilon \quad W_P \not\subset W_S}
        {[\,R/W_P\,]\,W_S \;\to\; W_S}
        \and
        (\mathrm{call}_{v})\;\; \frac{}{f_j(W_1, \dots, W_{m_j}) \;\to\; B_j[X_i := W_i]}
        \and
        (\mathrm{call}_{n})\;\; \frac{}{f_j(E_1, \dots, E_{m_j}) \;\to\; B_j[X_i := E_i]}
    \end{mathpar}
    Concatenation and firing are shared; $\mathrm{inert}_{=}$ (all three
    slots values -- the pass strict in every part) and $\mathrm{call}_{v}$
    (call by value) are the \emph{eager} rules;
    $\mathrm{inert}_{\epsilon}$ -- the replacement an arbitrary,
    discarded expression, which is the operator equation
    $[\,A/B\,]S = S$ of Definition~\ref{def:subst} made operational --
    and $\mathrm{call}_{n}$ the \emph{lazy-pass} rules.  The evaluation
    contexts are the whole difference.  \emph{Eager}: the hole may sit in
    any child of a concatenation, in any slot of a pass, in any argument
    of a call.  \emph{Lazy passes}: any child of a concatenation,
    the pattern or scrutinee slot of a pass, and the replacement slot
    only under the \emph{guard} that the other two slots are values and
    the pattern occurs in the scrutinee's value.  A run halting at a
    string denotes it; a closed non-value admitting no step is
    \emph{stuck} (an empty pattern, or a forced stuck sub-expression);
    otherwise it diverges.  The runtime's partial function is defined
    exactly on the halting runs.
\end{definition}

\begin{remark}
    \label{rem:dynamics}
    The dynamics are the two runtimes of the list, made precise: the
    eager relation computes the least-fixpoint denotation of
    Theorem~\ref{thm:eager}, and the
    lazy-pass relation is the machine of Proposition~\ref{prop:lpcons}.
    The rules fix \emph{what} is forced, not the order: the value and
    the defined inputs are order-independent in both runtimes, while
    the split of the undefined runs into stuck and divergent is not
    (Theorem~\ref{thm:eager} records this for eager).  The replacement
    slot's guard is a neededness criterion in the sense of Huet and
    L\'evy \cite{huetlevy79}: the replacement of $[\,R/P\,]\,E$ is needed
    exactly when $P$ occurs.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Eager Evaluation: Recursion Is Inert}

\begin{theorem}
    (\emph{Eager inertness})
    \label{thm:eager}
    Over any finite alphabet: if $j \in C$ then $\llbracket f_j \rrbracket$ is undefined on every input, whatever the bodies contain, and no run of $f_j$ ever halts with a value (an error, or an infinite descent; both modes occur). If $j \notin C$ then no run of $f_j$ ever diverges -- every input ends in a value or an error -- and $\llbracket f_j \rrbracket = \llbracket E_j \rrbracket$ for a plain expression $E_j \in \mathrm{Exp}_{m_j}$, the strict inlining of the acyclic calls. The eager recursive calculus denotes exactly the partial functions of $L$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
    Every constructor of Definition~\ref{def:den} is strict in all its sub-values, so undefinedness at any evaluated position propagates to the root. If $j \in C$, some call node of $B_j$ reaches a definition of $C$, and a Kleene induction gives $\varphi^n_j = \bot$ for every $n$: the callee's approximant is everywhere $\bot$, and strictness carries $\bot$ from the node through the body -- for every choice of bodies and every input. (The denotational and operational readings agree throughout; the Err/Div split is evaluation-order-dependent, the Val/never-Val split is not.) If $j \notin C$, the region of the call graph below $j$ is a DAG of depth $D(j)$, and an induction on call-depth shows that no evaluation with fuel $\geq D(j)$ is ever cut short: no input diverges. Were some input to diverge, the tree of body entries would be infinite and finitely branching, so K\"onig's lemma would give an infinite branch -- an infinite walk in the finite call graph, which revisits a node, a cycle reachable from $j$. The inlining: unfold the DAG in topological order, each call node replaced by the callee's body with the arguments substituted. Lemma~\ref{lem:beta}'s occurrence clause is what makes the substitution delicate -- plain $\beta$-inlining is call-by-name flavored, while the eager call is strict in \emph{all} arguments, the dead ones included -- so each discarded argument is forced through the identity passes of Corollary~\ref{cor:closure}'s proof before it is dropped. Conversely $L$ is contained: the one-definition program with body $E$.
\end{proof}

Two consequences deserve the record. Since every eager denotation is an $L$ denotation, the whole theory of Sections~\ref{sec:calculus}--\ref{sec:limits} applies verbatim -- safety, pipeline normal form, the polynomial-time bound; and safety can be read off the program directly ($j \notin C$, safe bodies). And the failure modes are now separated by a graph property: membership in $C$ is reachability, decidable in linear time -- a definition in $C$ is incapacitated \emph{by the recursion itself}, on every input and for every body, while outside $C$ undefinedness is the inlining's own, data-dependent. (The inlining can be exponentially larger than the program: expressiveness is unaffected, but the naive interpreter need not run in the polynomial time of the function it computes; memoization restores it.)

\subsection{Lazy Passes: Universality}
\label{sec:rec-lazy}

Widening the operator's own non-strictness changes everything. On call-free programs the lazy-pass machine is conservative:

\begin{proposition}
    (\emph{Conservativity})
    \label{prop:lpcons}
    On programs without calls, the lazy-pass machine computes the eager denotation wherever that is defined, and is strictly more defined only through discarded replacements.
\end{proposition}

The strictness that makes recursion inert is the strictness of the \emph{scrutinee}: a pass $[\,R/P\,]\,E$ needs $P$ and $E$, but needs $R$ only when it fires. Under lazy passes that is the whole story, and recursion through the replacement slot becomes conditional -- a gate. A gate that is sound both ways takes care:

\begin{definition}
    (\emph{Two-way gate})
    \label{def:gate}
    Let $b \neq x$ in $\Sigma$ and $\top, \bot$ be as in Selection, and fix the constants $\mathbf{p} = bbx$ and $\mathbf{q} = xbb$ (both contain $bb$; neither occurs in the other). In the variables $X_1, X_2, X_3$,
    \begin{align}
        \mathtt{sel}(C, u, v) \;\triangleq\; \mathtt{dec}^2_{x}\big([\mathtt{enc}^2_{x}(X_3)/\mathbf{q}]\,[\mathtt{enc}^2_{x}(X_2)/\mathbf{p}]\;\mathtt{if}(X_1, \mathbf{p}, \mathbf{q})\big).
    \end{align}
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}
    (\emph{Gate})
    \label{thm:gate}
    For $C \in \{\top, \bot\}$ and any $u, v$: $\mathtt{sel}(\top, u, v) = u$ and $\mathtt{sel}(\bot, u, v) = v$, the taken branch's value verbatim; the machine forces the condition and exactly the taken branch, once.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
    By Selection, $\mathtt{if}(X_1, \mathbf{p}, \mathbf{q})$ is $\mathbf{p}$ or $\mathbf{q}$ as $X_1$ is $\top$ or $\bot$. If $C = \top$: the pass $[\mathtt{enc}^2(X_2)/\mathbf{p}]$ sees its pattern as its scrutinee, so it fires -- forcing $X_2$ -- and replaces the whole text by $\mathtt{enc}^2(u)$; the outer pass has pattern $\mathbf{q} = xbb \ni bb$, and $\mathtt{enc}^2$-images contain no $bb$ (Comma Code Lemma), so it is inert and $X_3$ is never forced. If $C = \bot$, symmetrically: $\mathbf{p} = bbx$ does not occur in $\mathbf{q} = xbb$, the $\mathbf{p}$-pass is inert, and the $\mathbf{q}$-pass fires and forces $X_3$. Either way $\mathtt{dec}^2_x \circ \mathtt{enc}^2_x = \mathrm{id}$ (Comma Code Lemma (i)) returns the branch verbatim.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}
    (\emph{The Selection was already a gate})
    \label{rem:ifgate}
    Section~\ref{sec:toolkit}'s construction places the branches only in \emph{replacement} slots: in $\mathtt{if}(C,X,Y)$, both $X$ and $Y$ travel inside the replacements $[\mathtt{enc}(X)/\top]$ and $[\mathtt{enc}(Y)/bb]$. Under lazy passes, $\mathtt{if}$ is therefore itself a two-way gate, and no new machinery is needed. Writing $\mathtt{isne}(S) \triangleq \mathtt{if}(\mathtt{eq}(S,\epsilon), \bot, \top)$ for the emptiness test,
    \begin{align}
        \mathtt{rev}(X) \;=\; \mathtt{if}\big(\mathtt{isne}\,X,\;\mathtt{cat}(\mathtt{rev}(\mathtt{tail}\,X), \mathtt{head}\,X),\;\epsilon\big)
    \end{align}
    reverses every string. This is also the minimal repair of the eager calculus's one visible totality defect: the guard that Remark~\ref{rem:total-rep} must work around -- an $\mathtt{if}$ on $\mathtt{eq}(X_i, \epsilon)$ whose dead branch holds the round -- becomes sound as written, the round being a discarded replacement when the gate is closed.
\end{remark}

\begin{example}
    (\emph{A naive gate is unsound})
    \label{ex:naivegate}
    The definition
    \begin{align*}
        F(X) \;\triangleq\; [\,F(\mathtt{tail}\,X)/a\,]\;\mathtt{if}(\mathtt{isne}\,X, a, a)
    \end{align*}
    \emph{looks} guarded -- the recursive call is a replacement, demanded only when the pattern $a$ occurs -- but the closed-gate value $a$ contains the gate pattern itself, and the pass fires inside the base value: $F(\epsilon)$ forces $F(\epsilon)$, and the intended base case is a loop. The two-way gate is immune by construction: the gate values are the constants $\mathbf{p}$ and $\mathbf{q}$, and the branch values travel as $\mathtt{enc}^2$-images, which no pattern of the construction can meet.
\end{example}

\begin{theorem}
    (\emph{Universality})
    \label{thm:universal}
    The lazy-pass recursive calculus over any alphabet with $|\Sigma| \geq 2$ computes exactly the partial computable functions $(\Sigma^*)^n \rightharpoonup \Sigma^*$. Self-recursion suffices; mutual recursion is definable from it.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
    Soundness is effectivity: each machine rule is primitive recursive in its data, so every denotation is partial computable. For completeness, work over a two-letter subalphabet $\{\mathtt{a}, \mathtt{b}\}$ (a fixed two-letter prefix code -- constant passes both ways, as in Section~\ref{sec:toolkit} -- reduces the general case to it). Code strings into $a$-tallies by Horner with a sentinel bit, $V(\epsilon) = a$, $V(cT) = a^{2V(T) + \mathrm{idx}(c)}$: injective, and computed by a structural recursion with $\mathtt{cat}$ and $\mathtt{head}$. Its inverse $\mathrm{DIGITS}$ recurses structurally as well, reading the parity with a flip and halving with the raw-$L$ pipeline $[\,a/b\,][\,\epsilon/a\,][\,b/aa\,]$ -- the mirror of the once-calculus's $H$ (Section~\ref{sec:variants}) -- so no new primitive is involved anywhere. By Minsky's theorem \cite{minsky67} there is a two-counter machine with $V(X) \mapsto V(f(X))$, and counter machines compile into guarded definitions: a configuration is the string $b^i\,a^{x+1}\,b\,a^{y+1}\,b$, each instruction is a bounded pattern scan with $\mathtt{eq}$-tests on the instruction counter, and the driver
    \begin{align}
        \mathrm{RUN}(c) \;=\; \mathtt{sel}\big(\mathtt{eq}(\mathtt{PC}(c), \epsilon),\; \mathtt{out}(c),\; \mathrm{RUN}(\mathtt{step}(c))\big)
    \end{align}
    is the pattern-gated while loop -- the recursive call is forced exactly while the machine has not halted. Then $f = \mathrm{DIGITS} \circ \mathrm{RUN} \circ V$. Mutual recursion is definable from self-recursion: one definition over tagged pairs, with the projections plain passes.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}
    (\emph{The two barriers dissolve})
    \label{cor:barriers}
    \begin{enumerate}
        \item \emph{Reversal is computable} -- the second hinge of Section~\ref{sec:variants}, open for $L$ itself, dissolves one level up: Remark~\ref{rem:ifgate}'s three-line recursion. The hinge question for $L$ -- whether reversal is reachable by a \emph{bounded pipeline} -- is what remains.
        \item $X \mapsto X^{2^{|X|}}$, unreachable in $L$ by Lemma~\ref{lem:length}, is the total structural recursion
        \begin{align}
            A(S, W) \;=\; \mathtt{sel}\big(\mathtt{isne}\,S,\; A(\mathtt{tail}\,S, \mathtt{cat}(W,W)),\; W\big)
        \end{align}
        with $\mathrm{EXP}(X) = A(X, X)$. The degree machinery of Section~\ref{sec:limits} is a statement about pipelines (Proposition~\ref{prop:pipeline}); recursion is not a pipeline.
        \item \emph{Totality does not tame it.} The two nodes of Proposition~\ref{prop:limgrowth}'s tower, rebuilt as gated while loops and iterated down the input by $\mathtt{tail}$, give a total $\mathrm{TWR}(S) = a^{K_{|S|}}$ with $K_0 = 4$ and $K_{n+1} = 2^{K_n/2+1}-2$ -- so $K_4 = 2^{128}-2$. Tower growth is thus available already \emph{inside the total recursive programs}, and the polynomial bound fails for total definitions, not merely divergent ones.
        \item \emph{Totality of a definition is undecidable} (a two-counter machine halts on its initial configuration iff the compiled definition is total; Theorem~\ref{thm:universal}).
    \end{enumerate}
\end{corollary}

Universality needed the gate, and the gate needs the loop: without self-reference, even the operator's own non-strictness leaves the reachable class exactly where it was. Proposition~\ref{prop:lpcons} located the call-free machine's extra definedness -- every point of it comes from a discarded replacement -- but did not say whether that definedness is \emph{realizable}: whether the lazy-pass denotation of a call-free expression is the partial function of some plain expression. It is, by an explicit translation.

Two small constructions carry it. First, the \emph{coercion}, fixing the two distinct characters $b, x \in \Sigma$ of Definition~\ref{def:gate}: $\gamma(X) \triangleq \mathtt{if}\big(\mathtt{eq}(X, \epsilon),\, b,\, X\big)$, total, with $\gamma(w) = w$ for $w \neq \epsilon$ and $\gamma(\epsilon) = b$ -- a pattern wrapped in $\gamma$ is never empty. Second, the \emph{occurrence test} for total values $U, V$:
\begin{align*}
    \mathtt{occ}(U,V) \;\triangleq\; \mathtt{if}\Big(&\mathtt{eq}(U, x),\;
        \mathtt{if}\big(\mathtt{eq}([\,xx/x\,]V,\, V),\, \bot,\, \top\big),\\
        &\mathtt{if}\big(\mathtt{eq}([\,x/\gamma(U)\,]V,\, V),\, \bot,\, \top\big)\Big),
\end{align*}
total, with value $\top$ iff $U$ occurs in $V$ whenever $U \neq \epsilon$ (the value at $U = \epsilon$ is defined but never load-bearing). Soundness is the observation that a pass whose replacement differs from its pattern changes its scrutinee iff the pattern occurs -- if the pattern is absent the text stands, and if it is present the spliced span, or the length, must move -- and the replacement $x$ differs from the pattern $\gamma(U)$ on the second branch by the case split itself, while the first branch's $[\,xx/x\,]$ strictly grows on firing. No freshness assumption is needed: the one degeneracy, pattern $=$ replacement, is routed around by testing $U$ first.

\begin{theorem}
    (\emph{Flat lazy passes denote $\mathrm{L}$.})
    \label{thm:flatlazy}
    For every call-free expression $E$ over any alphabet with $|\Sigma| \geq 2$ there is a plain expression $E^{+}$ with $\llbracket E^{+} \rrbracket = \llbracket E \rrbracket^{\mathrm{lp}}$ -- the same partial function, domain included -- and $E^{+}$ is polynomially larger than $E$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
    Write $\llbracket E \rrbracket^{\mathrm{lp}}$ for the lazy-pass denotation of the call-free $E$. Two mutually recursive translations, by structural induction. The \emph{value part} $\Phi(E)$ replaces every pattern by its coerced version: $\Phi(X_i) = X_i$, $\Phi(W) = W$, $\Phi(E_1 E_2) = \Phi(E_1)\Phi(E_2)$, and
    \begin{align*}
        \Phi\big(\,[\,R/P\,]\,T\,\big) \;=\; \big[\,\Phi R\,/\,\gamma(\Phi P)\,\big]\,\Phi T .
    \end{align*}
    Every pattern in $\Phi(E)$ is a $\gamma$-image, never empty, and every other position is total by induction, so $\Phi(E)$ is total. On an input where the lazy-pass machine defines $E$, the patterns are forced and nonempty, so each $\gamma$ acts as the identity there; an inert pass contributes its scrutinee either way (its replacement, total, is discarded); and a firing pass forces $R$, where $\Phi R$ agrees with $R$ by induction. Hence $\Phi(E)$ agrees with $\llbracket E \rrbracket^{\mathrm{lp}}$ on its domain.

    The \emph{domain part} $F(E) \in \{\top, \bot\}$, total, indicates the domain: $F(X_i) = F(W) = \top$; $F(E_1 E_2) = F(E_1) \wedge F(E_2)$; and
    \begin{align*}
        F\big(\,[\,R/P\,]\,T\,\big) \;=\;
        F(T) \,\wedge\, F(P) \,\wedge\, \mathtt{isne}(\Phi P)
        \,\wedge\, \big(\neg\,\mathtt{occ}(\Phi P, \Phi T) \;\vee\; F(R)\big),
    \end{align*}
    with $\mathtt{isne}$ as in Remark~\ref{rem:ifgate} and the connectives $\mathtt{if}$-combinations of $\top$ and $\bot$, all total -- note the \emph{conjunction}: an empty forced pattern must falsify $F$ outright, not vacuously satisfy it. The sub-expressions of $F$ are total -- the scrutinee and pattern of a node enter through $F$ itself, their \emph{values} only through the total $\Phi$ images -- so $F$ is well defined, and $F(E) = \top$ exactly on $\mathrm{dom}\big(\llbracket E \rrbracket^{\mathrm{lp}}\big)$: the node is defined iff its scrutinee and pattern are, the pattern is nonempty, and either the pattern is absent or the forced replacement is defined.

    Finally the \emph{guard}: $E^{+} \triangleq [\,b/\,\mathtt{if}(F(E),\, b,\, \epsilon)\,]\;\Phi(E)$. On the domain the pass is $[\,b/b\,]$, the identity; off it the pattern is empty and the expression is undefined. The size is a \emph{sum over the nodes, not a product over the levels}: $\Phi(E)$ is linear in $|E|$, and $F$ at a node adds $F$ of the children plus one copy each of the $\Phi$-images of the node's own pattern and scrutinee, so $|E^{+}| = O(|E|^2)$.

\end{proof}

The theorem completes Proposition~\ref{prop:lpcons} and sharpens the border this section draws. Eager evaluation leaves the reachable class unchanged even with recursion (Theorem~\ref{thm:eager}); lazy passes make recursion universal through the gate (Theorem~\ref{thm:universal}). Theorem~\ref{thm:flatlazy} shows that the evaluation order \emph{alone} -- the operator's own non-strictness, without a self-reference to guard -- never leaves $\mathrm{L}$: the gate needs the loop. Section~\ref{sec:variants}'s $\mathrm{lim}$ node is the same border seen from the far side: flat $\mathrm{L}$ plus one iteration node computes \emph{exactly} the partial computable functions (Corollary~\ref{cor:limclass}) -- the loop, with no recursion to unwind, is the whole of it. In the terms of Proposition~\ref{prop:lpcons}, every discarded replacement is not merely harmless but \emph{recoverable}: the definedness the eager reading would lose to it is exactly the definedness the guard re-inserts.

\subsection{The Landscape}

\begin{center}
\footnotesize
\begin{tabular}{l|l}
    runtime & denotes \\
    \hline
    eager & $L$ exactly -- recursion inert \\
    lazy passes & the partial computable functions
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

Both rows share one primitive; the distance between them is a single evaluation rule. What remains open:
\begin{enumerate}
    \item \emph{The borderline.} The replacement slot is the one non-strictness universality needs: eager evaluation gives nothing, it gives everything. Is there an intermediate discipline -- a graded forcing of the replacement, say -- with strictly intermediate power?
    \item \emph{Structural recursion only.} The schemes of Corollary~\ref{cor:barriers}(ii)--(iii) are structural -- each call on $\mathtt{tail}\,S$ -- and it is the $\mu$-gates that carry the universality. Restricting to structural recursion plausibly yields a string analogue of a subrecursive hierarchy; unexplored.
    \item \emph{Gates per variant.} The once-calculus's Selection splices its branches into the \emph{scrutinee} (Theorem~\ref{thm:once-toolkit}(v)), so it is not a gate under lazy passes; whether the once-primitive admits any two-way gate -- whether once-recursion is inert or universal -- is open, the recursive sibling of Section~\ref{sec:variants}'s first hinge.
\end{enumerate}

\section{Conclusion}

This note took search-and-replace seriously as a model of computation, and found a small theory with classical echoes. The single pass $[A/B]$ -- leftmost-first, all occurrences, no re-entry into inserted text -- supports a complete toolkit: escaping, concatenation, head and tail, equality, selection, and simultaneous multiple substitution are each an explicit composition of passes with a correctness proof, over any alphabet with at least two characters. The calculus built on the primitive is structurally well-behaved: a syntactic safety condition implies totality, concatenation is eliminable, and every reachable function is computable in polynomial time, with the degree read off the expression -- while quadratic growth is reachable and exponential growth is not. Varying the design choices one at a time separated the once-primitive from full passes, tied the right-to-left fragment to the reachability of reversal, and closed the iteration story with an exact class: iterating an expression to its fixed point, one node over a flat pipeline already computes every partial computable function, one constant pass under it already grows exponentially, its divergent orbits grow like Fibonacci, and, we conjecture, nothing grows beyond single-exponential -- proved for the entire exponential tier by affine potentials, each sweep draining a nonnegative potential by a fixed amount, with the base $|A| - |B| + 1$ made exact by four rule shapes. Admitting both directions at once reduced the union's collapse to a single three-argument function, the right pass itself -- which we conjecture unreachable, carried by a mechanism, residue routing, that the left-to-right frontier cannot perform. Varying the alphabet changed none of this up to conjugation: a comma-free coding carries every reachable function to its conjugate over any other alphabet of at least two letters, both ways, transfers all four scanning primitives verbatim, and makes each hinge a single question across alphabets -- while the unary edge splits, reachable from below by coding and structurally blocked from above, its class eventually polynomially bounded. Letting definitions recurse, finally, split the runtimes rather than the primitive: eager evaluation buys nothing -- every recursive definition either inlines to a plain expression or never returns -- while the operator's one non-strictness, the replacement of a pass evaluated only when the pattern occurs, carries the calculus to universality -- though, without recursion, it buys definedness, not power.

Two questions about the baseline itself carry the structure of the whole landscape: whether the once-primitive is $L$-reachable, and whether reversal is. The first is affirmative at its probe over two letters (Proposition~\ref{prop:del-leftmost}), and the marking reduction collapses what remains: every alphabet, constant needle or computed, is the single binary constant node $[\,\mathtt{babba}/\mathtt{baa}\,]_1$ on the marked texts, where the cell prefix -- the left end of the probe -- would already decide it (Theorem~\ref{thm:marking}, Corollary~\ref{cor:once-crux}, Proposition~\ref{prop:cellprefix}); the second is answered negatively -- sharpened by the union's criterion, which asks only whether the right pass as a function is reachable, priced by the crossing ladder of Remark~\ref{rem:price}, and mapped at the alphabet's two ends: impossible over unbounded alphabets at any depth, computable on every fixed separator structure over any finite alphabet, and at varying separator count closed -- no single expression for all of $\Sigma^*$, each expression failing on the strongly super-increasing inputs beyond a separator count linear in its pass count (Section~\ref{ssec:frontier}). Both are single questions, not one per alphabet: the once node and the right pass are coding-equivariant, and reversal over one alphabet of at least two letters is reachable iff over every (Section~\ref{sec:alphabet}) -- the negative proof needed only one. Around them, the exact characterization of each variant's reachable class and the growth dichotomy for the iterated one-rule fragment remain open; the one remaining hinge -- the once-primitive, one constant node over two letters -- is where the theory now asks to be settled by hand.
